| `mexico:01` | **Son Jarocho** | Sotavento Region, Veracruz | Afro-Indigenous-Spanish string ensemble music played on jarana, requinto jarocho, harp, and percussive tarima foot-stomping. |
| `mexico:02` | **Son Huasteco (Huapango)** | La Huasteca (Tamaulipas, Veracruz, San Luis Potosí, Hidalgo) | Virtuosic trio music featuring violin improvisation, huapanguera, jarana huasteca, and soaring falsetto vocal leaps. |
| `mexico:03` | **Son Istmeño** | Isthmus of Tehuantepec, Oaxaca | Slow, melancholic marimba, brass, and acoustic guitar waltzes (sandungas) sung in Zapotec and Spanish. |
| `mexico:04` | **Son Calentano** | Tierra Caliente (Michoacán, Guerrero, Estado de México) | Fiery, syncopated double-violin, guitarra de golpe, and tamborita music with intricate metric shifts. |
| `mexico:05` | **Son Abajeño (Pirekua & Purépecha String Music)** | Michoacán Highlands | Indigenous Purépecha vocal and string-band music played in flowing 3/8 and 6/8 meters with poetic nature lyricism. |
| `mexico:06` | **Mariachi Tradicional (Cocula / Jalisco String Style)** | Jalisco, Michoacán & Colima | Pre-trumpet rural string mariachi using vihuela, guitarrón, violins, and harp to play sones and jarabes. |
| `mexico:07` | **Banda Sinaloense (Tambora)** | Mazatlán & Culiacán, Sinaloa | Powerful 15-to-20-piece wind and brass band anchored by the tuba (sousaphone), clarinets, trumpets, and tarola snare rolls. |
| `mexico:08` | **Norteño (Música Norteña)** | Nuevo León, Tamaulipas & Chihuahua | Northern Mexican accordion and bajo sexto music built on European-derived polkas, redovas, and corrido narratives. |
| `mexico:09` | **Narcocorrido** | Sinaloa, Sonora & Northern Border | Ballad storytelling genre chronicling smuggling and underworld figures over driving norteño or banda arrangements. |
| `mexico:10` | **Corrido Tumbado (Trap Corrido)** | Hermosillo, Sonora & Guadalajara | 2020s fusion of acoustic twelve-string requinto guitar and tololoche slap-bass with hip-hop cadences and brass trombones. |
| `mexico:11` | **Sierreño (Campirano)** | Sierra Madre Occidental (Sinaloa, Durango, Sonora) | Stripped-down acoustic trio format featuring lead twelve-string requinto, rhythm guitar, and tuba or electric bass. |
| `mexico:12` | **Cumbia Sonidera** | Peñón de los Baños (Mexico City) & Puebla | Slowed-down, bass-heavy Mexican street sound-system cumbia featuring pitch-shifted accordion, guacharaca, and live DJ shout-outs (saludos). |
| `mexico:13` | **Cumbia Rebajada** | Monterrey, Nuevo León | Colombian accordion cumbia records played at reduced turntable speeds, creating a hypnotic, sludgy, low-register groove. |
| `mexico:14` | **Chilena Costeña** | Costa Chica (Guerrero & Oaxaca) | Afro-Mexican coastal dance music descended from Chilean cueca sailors' songs, played with brass bands or bajo quinto and cajón. |
| `mexico:15` | **Jarana Yucateca** | Yucatán Peninsula (Mérida & Campeche) | Festive Peninsular dance music blending Spanish jota/fandango meters with woodwind/brass charanga orchestras and Maya lyricism. |
| `mexico:16` | **Canto Cardenche** | Comarca Lagunera (Durango & Coahuila) | Haunting, unaccompanied three- and four-part polyphonic a cappella peasant songs named after the painful thorn of the cardenche cactus. |
| `mexico:17` | **Onda Chicana (1970s Mexican Psych-Funk-Rock)** | Mexico City, Tijuana & Monterrey | Early-1970s Avándaro-era Mexican psychedelic rock blending fuzz guitars, Hammond organ, brass horns, and Indigenous/Latin percussion. |
| `mexico:18` | **Rock Urbano Mexicano (Rupestre)** | Nezahualcóyotl & Ecatepec, Mexico City Periphery | Working-class barrio blues-rock and poetic urban folk chronicling street life on the edges of Mexico City. |
| `mexico:19` | **Avanzada Regia** | Monterrey, Nuevo León | Mid-1990s–2000s northern Mexican alternative fusion blending ska, hip-hop, cumbia, funk, and alternative rock. |
| `mexico:20` | **Tribal Guarachero (3Ball)** | Monterrey, Nuevo León & San Luis Potosí | Electronic club music fusing pre-Hispanic flute motifs and Afro-Colombian cumbia triplet rhythms with 130 BPM house beats. |
| `mexico:21` | **Duranguense (Pasito Duranguense)** | Durango & Chicago Diaspora | Fast-paced polka-band style replacing the acoustic tuba and accordion with Yamaha DX7/Korg synthesizer brass and saxophones. |
| `mexico:22` | **Tierra Caliente (Techno-Banda Calentana)** | Michoacán & Guerrero | High-pitched, romantic regional pop-band style pairing electric bass and drums with synthesizer flutes, trombones, and charchetas. |
| `mexico:23` | **Danzón Veracruzano** | Veracruz Port | Cuban-derived formal ballroom dance music adopted in Veracruz with lush wind, string, and timbale orchestras. |
| `mexico:24` | **Trova Yucateca (Bambuco Yucateco)** | Mérida, Yucatán | Early-20th-century romantic guitar trio serenade tradition influenced by Colombian bambuco and Cuban bolero. |
| `mexico:25` | **Marimba Chiapaneca** | Tuxtla Gutiérrez & San Cristóbal, Chiapas | Double-keyboard wooden marimba ensembles performing polyphonic sones, zapateados, and waltzes. |
| `mexico:26` | **Wixarika (Huichol) Violin & Kanari Folk-Cumbia** | Northern Jalisco & Nayarit Sierra | Indigenous Wixarika handmade lute (xaweri) and violin music adapted to ultra-fast regional cumbia and corrido rhythms. |
| `mexico:27` | **Banda Oaxaqueña (Filarmónica de Viento)** | Sierra Norte & Mixteca, Oaxaca | Community village wind-and-brass bands playing complex traditional jarabes, chilenas, and funeral/fiesta marches. |
| `mexico:28` | **Tijuana Nortec (Norteño-Techno)** | Tijuana, Baja California | Late-1990s borderland electronic style chopping live tuba, accordion, and tarola snare samples into minimal techno and IDM. |
| `mexico:29` | **Bolero Ranchero** | Mexico City & Jalisco | Mid-20th-century Golden Age cinema crossover pairing intimate romantic bolero melodies with lush mariachi trumpets and strings. |
| `mexico:30` | **Mexican Post-Punk & Darkwave (Escena Oscura)** | Mexico City & Guadalajara | 1980s–present underground coldwave and synth-punk driven by chorus-heavy bass, drum machines, and Spanish gothic poetry. |

| `central-america:12` | **Plena Panameña (Reggae en Español / Dancehall)** | Colón & Panama City, Panama | Late-1970s–80s Spanish-language adaptation of Jamaican dancehall riddims that laid the rhythmic foundation for reggaetón. |
| `central-america:13` | **Combo Nacional Panameño** | Panama City & Colón, Panama | 1960s–70s Afro-Panamanian urban street bands blending calypso, salsa, cumbia, and American soul-funk with electric organ and horns. |
| `central-america:14` | **Cumbia Salvadoreña (Chanchona & Orquesta)** | San Miguel, Usulután & San Salvador, El Salvador | Buoyant Salvadoran cumbia existing in both rural upright-bass string bands (chanchona) and brass-heavy urban dance orchestras. |
| `central-america:15` | **Guanacasteco Punto & Parrandera** | Guanacaste Province, Costa Rica | Syncopated 6/8 marimba and brass fiesta music characterized by playful vocal gritos (güipipía) and intricate zapateado rhythms. |
| `central-america:16` | **Calypso Limonense** | Puerto Limón, Costa Rica | Afro-Costa Rican English-Creole troubadour calypso played on acoustic guitar, banjolele, quijongo (musical bow), and congas. |
| `central-america:17` | **Costa Rican Chiqui-Chiqui** | San José, Costa Rica | 1980s tropical synthesizer dance craze blending cumbia, merengue, and calypso into bright keyboard-driven party hits. |
| `central-america:18` | **Miskito Tiun & Kuswa Guitar Song** | Mosquitia Region (Eastern Honduras & Northeastern Nicaragua) | Indigenous Miskito coastal balladry sung in Miskito language over gently picked acoustic guitar, mandolin, and turtle shell. |
| `central-america:19` | **Xuc Salvadoreño** | Cojutepeque & San Salvador, El Salvador | Upbeat 2/4 Salvadoran folk-dance rhythm created in the 1950s featuring reed instruments, brass, and heel-toe choreography. |
| `central-america:20` | **Cumbia Hondureña** | Tegucigalpa & San Pedro Sula, Honduras | Coastal and urban Honduran cumbia infused with Caribbean punta percussion and bright brass/keyboard arrangements. |
| `central-america:21` | **Guatemalan 1970s Garage-Psych & Ácido Rock** | Guatemala City, Guatemala | Late-1960s–70s Central American psychedelic rock combining heavy fuzz guitar, Hammond organ, and Latin-soul polyrhythms. |
| `central-america:22` | **Panamanian Cantadera (Mejorana & Décima)** | Central Provinces, Panama | Improvised ten-line poetic singing duels accompanied by the five-string mejoranera or socavón lute. |
| `central-america:23` | **Garifuna Hungu-Hungu & Chumba** | Roatán, Trujillo & Livingston (Honduras / Guatemala) | Circular three-beat sacred and secular Garifuna women's dance song styles featuring polyrhythmic drumming and call-and-response. |
| `central-america:24` | **Nicaraguan Canto Nuevo (Volcanto)** | Managua & León, Nicaragua | 1970s–80s revolutionary acoustic folk-rock blending son nica rhythms with poetic protest songwriting. |
| `central-america:25` | **Congo Panameño** | Costa Arriba & Costa Abajo, Colón, Panama | Afro-colonial coastal carnival theatre and percussion tradition featuring complex cross-rhythms, revirado language, and defiant choral singing. |
| `central-america:26` | **Cumbia Costarricense (Swing Criollo)** | San José, Costa Rica | Working-class Costa Rican dancehall cumbia variant tailored for the bouncy, acrobatic 'swing criollo' partner dance. |
| `central-america:27` | **Belizean Cununu & Wanaragua (Jankunu)** | Dangriga & Seine Bight, Belize | Masked ritual warrior dance music driven by brisk, intricate segunda and primero drum dialogues. |
| `central-america:28` | **Honduran Caramba & Sique** | Interior Highlands (Yoro, Intibucá & Lempira), Honduras | Rural Honduran string-and-percussion folk music featuring the caramba (one-string gourd musical bow) and fast 6/8 guitar strumming. |
| `central-america:29` | **Panamanian Murga** | Las Tablas & Chitré, Panama | Brass-and-percussion street carnival ensemble blending cumbia melodies with roaring trombones, trumpets, and snare drums. |
| `central-america:30` | **Central American Indietrónica & Tropical Bass** | San José, Guatemala City & Panama City | Contemporary electronic producers fusing marimba, punta, and cumbia percussion with deep sub-bass and glitchy synth design. |

## Research leads

| Route ID | Source label | Claimed origin / scene — verify | Claimed sonic profile — verify |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `caribbean:01` | **Kingston Dub (70s Roots Dub)** | Kingston, Jamaica | Studio-as-instrument remix art using mixing-board spring reverb, Roland Space Echo delay, and drum-and-bass track dropouts. |
| `caribbean:02` | **Rocksteady** | Kingston, Jamaica | 1966–1968 slow-tempo successor to ska emphasizing melodic electric basslines, soulful vocal harmonies, and unhurried offbeat guitar. |
| `caribbean:03` | **Early Digital Dancehall (Ragga / Sleng Teng Era)** | Kingston, Jamaica | Mid-1980s shift from live studio bands to Casio MT-40 keyboard presets, drum machines, and rapid-fire deejay toasting. |
| `caribbean:04` | **Mento** | Rural Parishes & Kingston, Jamaica | Acoustic Jamaican folk precursor to ska featuring rhumba box (marímbula), banjo, bamboo saxophone, and topical humor. |
| `caribbean:05` | **Nyabinghi** | Pinnacle & Kingston, Jamaica | Sacred Rastafari liturgical drumming built on three hand drums (thunder, funde, repeater) playing heartbeat-derived rhythms. |
| `caribbean:06` | **Trinidadian Calypso** | Port of Spain, Trinidad and Tobago | Witty, satirical carnival song tradition backed by horn sections, Venezuelan-influenced cuatro strings, and syncopated bass. |
| `caribbean:07` | **Soca (Soul of Calypso)** | Port of Spain, Trinidad and Tobago | 1970s–present high-energy carnival music fusing calypso melodies with East Indian tassa/dholak rhythms and funk/electronic bass. |
| `caribbean:08` | **Chutney Soca** | San Fernando & Chaguanas, Trinidad and Tobago | Indo-Caribbean crossover blending Bhojpuri folk melodies, dholak, and dhantal percussion with driving soca brass and drum machines. |
| `caribbean:09` | **Rapso** | Belmont & Laventille, Trinidad and Tobago | Conscious Trinidadian street poetry and rhythmic chant movement merging calypso cadence with militant percussion. |
| `caribbean:10` | **Haitian Compas Direct (Konpa)** | Port-au-Prince, Haiti | Mid-1950s–present urban dance music driven by clean, arpeggiated electric guitar (twoubadou/jazz), cowbell/tom merengue pulse, and horn riffs. |
| `caribbean:11` | **Rara** | Artibonite & Port-au-Prince, Haiti | Lenten street-procession music powered by interlocking single-note bamboo/metal vaccine trumpets, rara whistles, and vodou percussion. |
| `caribbean:12` | **Rasin (Mizik Rasin)** | Port-au-Prince, Haiti | Late-1970s–90s roots movement fusing sacred Haitian Vodou ceremonial drumming and rara horns with psychedelic rock guitar and synth. |
| `caribbean:13` | **Zouk (Zouk Béton & Zouk Love)** | Guadeloupe & Martinique | 1980s French Antillean studio revolution combining gwo ka/bélé rhythms with high-tech Paris synth programming, slap bass, and brass. |
| `caribbean:14` | **Gwoka** | Guadeloupe | Antillean emancipated drum-and-voice tradition built on seven distinct rhythms played on boula and makè goatskin barrel drums. |
| `caribbean:15` | **Bélé (Bel Air)** | Sainte-Marie, Martinique | Call-and-response rural drum, tibwa (bamboo sticks), and dance tradition rooted in plantation gathering rituals. |
| `caribbean:16` | **Cadence-Lypso** | Roseau, Dominica | 1970s synthesis of Haitian cadence rampa and Trinidadian calypso with heavy electric bass, horn arrangements, and Creole lyrics. |
| `caribbean:17` | **Bouyon** | Dominica | Late-1980s–present high-tempo Dominican fusion of jing ping accordion folk, cadence-lypso, and digital dancehall. |
| `caribbean:18` | **Dennery Segment (Lucian Kuduro)** | Dennery, Saint Lucia | Fast, minimalist electronic club music combining Kwéyòl vocals with pounding Angolan kuduro-derived beats. |
| `caribbean:19` | **Spouge** | Bridgetown, Barbados | Late-1960s–70s Barbadian soul-calypso hybrid created by Jackie Opel, marked by cowbell backbeats, organ, and brass. |
| `caribbean:20` | **Tuk Band** | Barbados | Fife-and-drum masquerade music blending British military march instrumentation (pennywhistle, snare, bass drum) with African polyrhythms. |
| `caribbean:21` | **Bahamian Goombay & Rake-and-Scrape** | Nassau & Cat Island, Bahamas | Island folk and dance music built around goatskin goombay drums, carpenter's saw scraped with a nail, and button accordion. |
| `caribbean:22` | **Compass Point Electro-Boogie** | Nassau, Bahamas | Early-1980s studio sound fusing Jamaican Sly & Robbie reggae rhythm section with New Wave synths and NYC post-disco. |
| `caribbean:23` | **Cuban Son Montuno** | Oriente (Santiago de Cuba) & Havana, Cuba | Foundational Afro-Cuban song-and-improvisation form driven by tres guitar, upright bass tumbao, bongos, and clave. |
| `caribbean:24` | **Cuban Timba** | Havana, Cuba | 1990s hyper-virtuosic Cuban dance music fusing songo and rumba guaguancó with jazz-funk slap bass and complex gear-shift breaks. |
| `caribbean:25` | **Changüí** | Guantánamo Province, Cuba | Archaic eastern Cuban rural precursor to son featuring tres, marímbula, bongo de monte, guayo scraper, and syncopated steps. |
| `caribbean:26` | **Puerto Rican Bomba** | Loíza, Ponce & Mayagüez, Puerto Rico | Improvised dialogue between a solo dancer and the primo (subidor) barrel drummer, backed by maraca, cuá sticks, and chorus. |
| `caribbean:27` | **Puerto Rican Plena** | Ponce, Puerto Rico | Sung 'street newspaper' topical music driven by three sizes of handheld pandereta frame drums and accordion or horns. |
| `caribbean:28` | **Dominican Merengue Típico (Perico Ripiao)** | Cibao Valley (Santiago), Dominican Republic | Fast-paced rural merengue played on two-row button accordion, double-headed tambora drum, metal güira scraper, and marímbula/sax. |
| `caribbean:29` | **Dominican Bachata** | Santo Domingo & Rural Cibao, Dominican Republic | Bittersweet guitar bolero sibling characterized by syncopated, chorus-drenched lead requinto guitar arpeggios and bongo rolls. |
| `caribbean:30` | **Tumba & Tambú** | Curaçao, Aruba & Bonaire | Papiamentu-language Afro-Curaçaoan carnival and sacred percussion music featuring tambú drum, wiri metal scraper, and brass. |

| Route ID | Source label | Claimed origin / scene — verify | Claimed sonic profile — verify |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `northern-south-america:01` | **Peruvian Chicha (Cumbia Andina)** | Lima & Huancayo, Peru | Late-1960s–80s urban migrant fusion of Andean pentatonic huayno melodies, Colombian cumbia rhythms, and reverb-drenched surf-psych guitars. |
| `northern-south-america:02` | **Cumbia Amazónica** | Iquitos, Moyobamba & Pucallpa, Peruvian Amazon | Tropical jungle psychedelia featuring fuzz-wah electric guitars, Farfisa organs, upbeat timbales, and Amazonian folklore themes. |
| `northern-south-america:03` | **Festejo Afroperuano** | Chincha, Cañete & Lima, Peru | Festive Afro-Peruvian coastal rhythm played on wooden cajón, donkey jawbone (quijada), cajita, and syncopated acoustic guitar. |
| `northern-south-america:04` | **Landó** | Central Coast (Lima & Chincha), Peru | Sensual, hypnotic 12/8 Afro-Peruvian groove anchored by deep cajón bass tones, fretless bass, and expressive melancholic vocals. |
| `northern-south-america:05` | **Huayno (Wayno)** | Peruvian & Bolivian Andes (Cusco, Ayacucho, Puno) | Foundational Andean dance-song genre in brisk duple meter featuring charango, Andean harp, quena flute, and high-register vocals. |
| `northern-south-america:06` | **Danza de las Tijeras (Scissors Dance Music)** | Ayacucho, Huancavelica & Apurímac, Peru | Ritual Andean competition music pairing violin and indigenous harp with the metallic rhythmic clashing of iron rods held by dancers. |
| `northern-south-america:07` | **Vals Criollo Peruano** | Lima & Coastal Peru | Syncopated 3/4 coastal Peruvian waltz featuring virtuosic twin nylon-string guitars (punteo and rasgueo) and cajón accompaniment. |
| `northern-south-america:08` | **Yaraví** | Arequipa & Southern Peruvian Andes | Slow, mournful mestizo song form derived from Inca harawi laments, performed in minor keys with delicate guitar duets. |
| `northern-south-america:09` | **Colombian Champeta (Terapia Criolla)** | Cartagena & San Basilio de Palenque, Colombia | Afro-Colombian picó sound-system dance music blending Congolese soukous/mbaqanga guitar lines with digital percussion and local slang. |
| `northern-south-america:10` | **Currulao** | Pacific Coast (Chocó, Buenaventura, Tumaco), Colombia & Esmeraldas, Ecuador | Afro-Pacific 6/8 ritual and social music centered on the wooden marimba de chonta, cununo drums, tambora, and guasá shakers. |
| `northern-south-america:11` | **Bullerengue** | Urabá, Bolívar & Córdoba (Caribbean Coast), Colombia | Matriarchal Afro-Colombian call-and-response vocal and drum tradition led by a female cantadora, tambor alegre, and llamador. |
| `northern-south-america:12` | **Cumbia Colombiana (Gaita & Millo Tradition)** | Montes de María & Magdalena River, Colombia | Indigenous-African courtship rhythm combining long gaita flutes or caña de millo reed pipes with tambor alegre, llamador, and maracón. |
| `northern-south-america:13` | **Porro (Porro Sabanero / Pelayero)** | Sinú River Valley (Córdoba & Sucre), Colombia | Festive wind-and-brass band music mixing clarinet counter-melodies, trumpets, euphonium (bombardino), and redoblante snare rolls. |
| `northern-south-america:14` | **Vallenato** | Valledupar, Cesar & La Guajira, Colombia | Storytelling trio music built around the diatonic button accordion, scraped guacharaca cane, and handheld caja vallenata drum. |
| `northern-south-america:15` | **Bambuco** | Andean Interior (Cauca, Antioquia, Santander), Colombia | Syncopated polyrhythmic 3/4 and 6/8 string music played on tiple (12-string chordophone), bandola, and acoustic guitar. |
| `northern-south-america:16` | **Joropo Llanero (Música Llanera)** | Orinoco Plains (Venezuela & Eastern Colombia) | High-speed polyrhythmic 3/4–6/8 plains music driven by arpa llanera (harp) or bandola, cuatro, maracas, and declamatory vocals. |
| `northern-south-america:17` | **Gaita Zuliana** | Maracaibo, Zulia State, Venezuela | Afro-Venezuelan Christmas and protest music featuring the furro (friction drum), tambora, charrasca scraper, cuatro, and soaring chorus. |
| `northern-south-america:18` | **Tambor Venezolano (Sangueo & Golpe de Barlovento)** | Barlovento, Aragua & Vargas Coast, Venezuela | Polyrhythmic Afro-Venezuelan coastal drum suites utilizing cumaco, mina, and culo'e puya battery drums for Saint John festivals. |
| `northern-south-america:19` | **Onda Nueva Venezolana** | Caracas, Venezuela | Late-1960s–70s avant-garde synthesis created by Aldemaro Romero fusing Venezuelan joropo rhythms with Brazilian bossa nova and jazz. |
| `northern-south-america:20` | **Calipso de El Callao** | El Callao, Bolívar State, Venezuela | Gold-mining town carnival music sung in English-Creole and Spanish over bum-bac cylindrical drums, cuatro, and iron bells. |
| `northern-south-america:21` | **Bomba del Chota** | Chota Valley (Imbabura & Carchi), Ecuador | Afro-Ecuadorian highland valley dance music combining bongo-like bomba drum, requinto guitar, guiro, and Andean pentatonic melodies. |
| `northern-south-america:22` | **Pasillo Ecuatoriano** | Quito & Guayaquil, Ecuador | Melancholic, slow-tempo minor-key waltz derivative featuring weeping requinto guitar counter-melodies and romantic poetry. |
| `northern-south-america:23` | **Sanjuanito** | Otavalo & Imbabura Highlands, Ecuador | Pre-Columbian-rooted festive 2/4 Andean dance rhythm played on rondador panpipes, bandolín, charango, and bombo drum. |
| `northern-south-america:24` | **Carimbó** | Marajó Island & Belém, Pará, Northern Brazil | Amazonian coastal circle-dance music driven by hollowed tree-trunk curimbó drums, maracas, banjo, and flute/brass. |
| `northern-south-america:25` | **Guitarrada (Lambada Paraense)** | Belém & Abaetetuba, Pará, Northern Brazil | Instrumental Amazonian guitar music blending Caribbean cumbia, merengue, and carimbó with clean, tropical Fender guitar leads. |
| `northern-south-america:26` | **Tecnobrega (Tecnomelody)** | Belém, Pará, Northern Brazil | Aparelhagem sound-system electronic pop remixing romantic brega ballads with high-tempo drum machines and pitch-shifted synths. |
| `northern-south-america:27` | **Kaseko** | Paramaribo, Suriname | Complex Afro-Surinamese creole big-band dance music featuring skratji (large bass drum with cymbal), snare rolls, and call-and-response brass. |
| `northern-south-america:28` | **Kawina** | Suriname & French Guiana | Roots percussion-and-vocal precursor to kaseko utilizing interlocking hand drums, kwatro string instrument, and Sranan Tongo vocals. |
| `northern-south-america:29` | **Aleke** | Maroni River Basin, Suriname & French Guiana | Ndyuka Maroon youth drum-and-vocal style combining traditional Cottica River rhythms with modern urban dancehall cadences. |
| `northern-south-america:30` | **Guyanese Shanto & Chutney-Mashramani** | Georgetown & Berbice, Guyana | Syncopated Guyanese vaudeville-calypso (shanto) and Indo-Guyanese dholak/harmonium party styles performed during Mashramani. |

| `southern-south-america:01` | **Tropicália (Tropicalismo)** | Salvador, Bahia & São Paulo, Brazil | Late-1960s avant-garde Brazilian movement colliding bossa nova and nordestino folk with fuzz-psych guitars, concrete poetry, and orchestral pop. |
| `southern-south-america:02` | **Clube da Esquina (Minas Gerais Baroque-Folk-Jazz)** | Belo Horizonte, Minas Gerais, Brazil | 1970s harmonic movement blending modal jazz chords, Beatlesque art-rock, Iberian sacred polyphony, and rural Minas folk rhythms. |
| `southern-south-america:03` | **Manguebeat** | Recife & Olinda, Pernambuco, Brazil | 1990s Northeastern Brazilian countercultural explosion fusing regional maracatu alfaia drums and embolada with funk-metal, hip-hop, and dub. |
| `southern-south-america:04` | **Forró Pé-de-Serra (Baião & Xote)** | Sertão of Pernambuco, Paraíba & Ceará, Brazil | Rural Northeastern accordion (sanfona), zabumba bass drum, and metal triangle trio music driven by syncopated baião pulses. |
| `southern-south-america:05` | **Frevo** | Recife & Olinda, Pernambuco, Brazil | Breakneck, acrobatic carnival march played by brass fanfare bands (frevo de rua) or electric guitarra baiana (frevo elétrico). |
| `southern-south-america:06` | **Maracatu Nação (Baque Virado)** | Recife, Pernambuco, Brazil | Afro-Brazilian Candomblé-linked royal procession music powered by massive alfaia rope-tuned bass drums, gonguê bell, and xequerê. |
| `southern-south-america:07` | **Samba de Roda** | Recôncavo Baiano, Bahia, Brazil | Traditional Afro-Bahian circle samba featuring viola machete, pandeiro, prato-e-faca (plate and knife), and palm clapping. |
| `southern-south-america:08` | **Bossa Nova** | Rio de Janeiro, Brazil | Late-1950s intimate coastal style marrying whisper-quiet vocals and syncopated nylon-string guitar thumb-and-chord patterns with cool jazz harmonies. |
| `southern-south-america:09` | **Brazilian Boogie & 80s MPB Synth-Funk** | Rio de Janeiro & São Paulo, Brazil | Late-1970s–80s urban Brazilian studio funk combining slap bass, Clavinet, Rhodes, horn sections, and swinging samba-soul grooves. |
| `southern-south-america:10` | **Funk Carioca (Baile Funk / Tamborzão)** | Favelas of Rio de Janeiro, Brazil | Miami bass-derived favela club music evolved around the syncopated Afro-Brazilian 'Tamborzão' drum loop and MC vocal montages (montagem). |
| `southern-south-america:11` | **São Paulo Post-Punk & Vanguarda Paulista** | São Paulo, Brazil | Early-1980s angular urban post-punk and satirical avant-garde jazz-theatre emerging from São Paulo's independent Lira Paulistana scene. |
| `southern-south-america:12` | **Música Gaúcha (Chamamé & Milonga Sulina)** | Rio Grande do Sul, Southern Brazil | Pampas borderland accordion (gaita-ponto) and acoustic guitar music sharing roots with Argentine and Uruguayan gaucho folklore. |
| `southern-south-america:13` | **Argentine Rock Nacional (Spinetta / Charly García Era)** | Buenos Aires, Argentina | Late-1960s–80s Argentine progressive, symphonic, and jazz-rock movement distinguished by sophisticated harmony and literary Spanish lyrics. |
| `southern-south-america:14` | **Tango Nuevo** | Buenos Aires, Argentina | Mid-20th-century revolution led by Astor Piazzolla integrating bandoneón tango with Bach counterpoint, Stravinsky dissonance, and jazz improvisation. |
| `southern-south-america:15` | **Milonga Campera & Milonga Ciudadana** | Buenos Aires & Argentine Pampas | Syncopated habanera-pulse precursor to tango played either as slow, reflective rural guitar music or brisk urban dance-hall arrangements. |
| `southern-south-america:16` | **Chacarera** | Santiago del Estero, Argentina | Polyrhythmic 6/8 against 3/4 northwestern Argentine folk dance driven by the deep bombo legüero hide drum, violin, and acoustic guitars. |
| `southern-south-america:17` | **Zamba Argentina** | Salta & Tucumán, Northwestern Argentina | Graceful, melancholic 6/8 handkerchief courtship dance song characterized by poetic lyricism, bombo legüero, and rich vocal harmonies. |
| `southern-south-america:18` | **Chamamé** | Corrientes & Entre Ríos (Littoral Region), Argentina | Guarani-and-Spanish Mesopotamian folk style pairing two-row button accordion and bandoneón with syncopated 6/8 guitar strumming and sapucay cries. |
| `southern-south-america:19` | **Cuarteto Cordobés** | Córdoba, Argentina | Upbeat working-class dance-hall music propelled by the percussive 'tunga-tunga' piano-and-bass rhythm, accordion, and brass section. |
| `southern-south-america:20` | **Cumbia Villera** | Gran Buenos Aires Conurbano, Argentina | Early-2000s shantytown cumbia replacing brass with cheap keytar synthesizer leads, electronic octapad percussion, and raw social commentary. |
| `southern-south-america:21` | **Cumbia Santafesina** | Santa Fe, Argentina | Melodic Argentine cumbia subgenre where clean, weeping Fender Stratocaster electric guitar lines or bandoneón carry the primary melody. |
| `southern-south-america:22` | **Uruguayan Candombe & Canto Popular / Candombe Beat** | Barrio Sur & Palermo, Montevideo, Uruguay | Afro-Uruguayan three-drum (chico, repique, piano) street procession rhythm fused in the late 1960s–70s with electric jazz-rock and funk. |
| `southern-south-america:23` | **Murga Uruguaya** | Montevideo, Uruguay | Carnival musical theatre featuring a 13-to-17-voice nasal male/mixed choir backed by bombo, redoblante snare, and platillos cymbals. |
| `southern-south-america:24` | **Chilean Nueva Canción & Andean Avant-Folk** | Santiago, Valparaíso & Concepción, Chile | 1960s–70s socially committed folk movement uniting charango, quena, and bombo legüero with modernist poetry and harmonic experimentation. |
| `southern-south-america:25` | **Cueca Brava (Cueca Chora)** | Valparaíso Port & Estación Central (Santiago), Chile | Gritty urban port and brothel variant of the Chilean cueca sung in rapid, jazz-scat-like polyphony with piano, accordion, and pandero. |
| `southern-south-america:26` | **Cumbia Chilena (Sound & Nueva Cumbia Chilena)** | Santiago & Coquimbo, Chile | Horn-heavy, ska- and rock-inflected Chilean party cumbia blending Andean bronces with festive pachanga rhythms. |
| `southern-south-america:27` | **Paraguayan Polca Paraguaya (Purashei)** | Asunción & Guairá, Paraguay | Polyrhythmic Guarani-Spanish music stacking a 6/8 melody on the 36-string Paraguayan harp and guitars over a 3/4 bass pulse. |
| `southern-south-america:28` | **Guarania** | Asunción, Paraguay | Slow, minor-key orchestral and vocal ballad form created by José Asunción Flores in the 1920s to express Guarani lyricism. |
| `southern-south-america:29` | **Bolivian Morenada & Caporales** | Oruro & La Paz, Bolivia | Massive highland carnival brass-band music featuring rattling matraca percussion (Morenada) or energetic cascabeles bells and panpipes (Caporales). |
| `southern-south-america:30` | **Bolivian K'alampeo & Norte Potosí Charango** | Northern Potosí & Oruro Highlands, Bolivia | Indigenous Quechua-Aymara charango strumming style using metallic strings, microtonal intervals, and intense percussive rasgueos. |

# END SOURCE: references/regions/southern-south-america.md

Source descriptions are preserved below as unverified leads, including combined labels and named touchstones. This import does not independently substantiate their claims.

## Research leads

| Route ID | Source label | Claimed origin / scene — verify | Claimed sonic profile — verify |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `atlantic-europe:01` | **Krautrock / Kosmische Musik** | Düsseldorf, Cologne, West Berlin & Munich, West Germany | Hypnotic 4/4 motorik drumming, modular Moog/EMS synthesizer sequences, tape manipulation, and extended experimental improvisation. |
| `atlantic-europe:02` | **Neue Deutsche Welle (NDW)** | Düsseldorf, Hamburg, West Berlin & Hanover, West Germany | Late-1970s–early-1980s German post-punk and minimal synth-pop marked by angular rhythms, Korg MS-20 basslines, and dadaist German lyrics. |
| `atlantic-europe:03` | **Brussels New Beat** | Brussels, Ghent & Antwerp, Belgium | Late-1980s Belgian club sound created by pitching 33/45 RPM EBM and acid records down to ~95–115 BPM for heavy, ominous grooves. |
| `atlantic-europe:04` | **Belgian EBM (Electronic Body Music)** | Brussels, Belgium | Early-1980s industrial dance music combining sequenced octave synth basslines, gated-reverb electronic drums, and barked vocals. |
| `atlantic-europe:05` | **French Coldwave** | Paris, Lyon, Rouen & Lille, France | Late-1970s–80s francophone post-punk utilizing chorus-soaked bass guitars, stark CR-78/LinnDrum beats, and detached, melancholic vocals. |
| `atlantic-europe:06` | **French Cosmic Library Music (Musique d'Illustration)** | Paris, France (Patchwork / Tele Music / Musax Studios) | Late-1960s–70s broadcast/cinema production cues blending Clavinet funk, analog ARP/Moog synths, and baroque basslines. |
| `atlantic-europe:07` | **Zeuhl** | Paris, France | 1970s avant-prog/jazz-rock movement founded by Christian Vander (Magma), featuring operatic Kobaïan-language choirs, fuzz bass, and martial rhythms. |
| `atlantic-europe:08` | **Bal-Musette** | Paris (Bastille / Belleville), France | Early-20th-century Parisian dance-hall waltz and java style centered on the chromatic button accordion, banjo, and cabrette bagpipe. |
| `atlantic-europe:09` | **Jazz Manouche (Gypsy Jazz)** | Paris, France & Romani Sinti Communities | 1930s acoustic swing pioneered by Django Reinhardt using Selmer-Maccaferri guitars, 'la pompe' rhythm strumming, and violin. |
| `atlantic-europe:10` | **Breton Fest-Noz (Kan ha Diskan & Bagad)** | Brittany (Breizh), Northwestern France | Celtic nocturnal circle-dance music pairing overlapping call-and-response vocals (kan ha diskan) with bombardes and biniou kozh bagpipes. |
| `atlantic-europe:11` | **French Touch (Filter House)** | Paris & Versailles, France | 1990s house subgenre built on heavily sidechained, low-pass-filtered 1970s disco and funk loops over punchy 4/4 kick drums. |
| `atlantic-europe:12` | **Bristol Trip-Hop (Bristol Sound)** | Bristol, England | Early-1990s sound-system fusion of slowed-down hip-hop breakbeats, deep Jamaican dub basslines, cinematic strings, and hushed vocals. |
| `atlantic-europe:13` | **UK Lovers Rock** | South & West London, England | Mid-1970s–80s British-born reggae subgenre merging Jamaican rocksteady/roots rhythms with silky Chicago and Philadelphia soul vocal harmonies. |
| `atlantic-europe:14` | **UK Street Soul** | London, Bristol, Manchester & Nottingham, England | Late-1980s–early-1990s DIY British R&B combining Soul II Soul-style breakbeats, dub bass, and raw inner-city soul vocals. |
| `atlantic-europe:15` | **Sheffield Bleep & Bass (Bleep Techno)** | Sheffield, Leeds & Bradford, Yorkshire, England | 1989–1991 Northern English rave sound pairing high-pitched futuristic synth bleeps with rattling sound-system sub-bass. |
| `atlantic-europe:16` | **UK Jungle (Ragga Jungle)** | London, England | Early-1990s rave music slicing pitched-up ~160–170 BPM 'Amen' breakbeats over seismic half-time dub basslines and dancehall MCing. |
| `atlantic-europe:17` | **UK Garage (2-Step Garage)** | London, England | Late-1990s club style removing the first and third four-on-the-floor kicks for a shuffling, syncopated 2-step swing and chopped R&B vocals. |
| `atlantic-europe:18` | **Bow E3 Grime (8-Bar Grime)** | East London (Bow, Mile End, Hackney), England | Early-2000s 140 BPM pirate-radio electronic rap built on raw FruityLoops square-wave bass, pizzicato strings, and rapid MC clash bars. |
| `atlantic-europe:19` | **South London Dubstep (Croydon 140)** | Croydon & South London, England | Early-2000s dark, spacious 140 BPM bass music emphasizing sub-bass weight, sparse half-time snares, and cavernous dub echo. |
| `atlantic-europe:20` | **Madchester (Baggy)** | Manchester, England | Late-1980s indie-dance crossover marrying 1960s jangly psychedelic guitar rock with acid house loops and shuffling 'Funky Drummer' beats. |
| `atlantic-europe:21` | **Thames Valley Shoegaze** | Reading, Oxford & London, England | Late-1980s–early-1990s indie rock using reverse reverb, glide-guitar tremolo arms, and fuzz pedals to bury whispered vocals in guitar wash. |
| `atlantic-europe:22` | **Canterbury Scene** | Canterbury, Kent, England | Late-1960s–70s eccentric English progressive rock and jazz-fusion defined by fuzz-toned Hammond organ, whimsical lyricism, and odd meters. |
| `atlantic-europe:23` | **British Folk-Baroque (Davy Graham / Soho Revival)** | London, England | 1960s fingerstyle acoustic guitar movement using DADGAD tuning to blend traditional British ballads with blues, jazz, and Indian/Moroccan modes. |
| `atlantic-europe:24` | **Scottish Waulking Song & Gaelic Psalm Singing** | Outer Hebrides (Lewis, Harris, Barra), Scotland | Rhythmic Scottish Gaelic communal cloth-fulling work songs and heterophonic, unconducted Free Church Gaelic psalm lining-out. |
| `atlantic-europe:25` | **Scottish Bothy Ballads** | North-East Scotland (Aberdeenshire & Moray) | Unaccompanied or melodeon-backed narrative farm-servant songs sung in Doric Scots dialect. |
| `atlantic-europe:26` | **Irish Sean-nós Singing** | Connemara, Donegal & West Kerry Gaeltacht, Ireland | Highly ornamented, unaccompanied traditional Irish-language solo singing characterized by microtonal melisma and free rhythm. |
| `atlantic-europe:27` | **Sliabh Luachra Fiddle & Polka-Slide Tradition** | Cork, Kerry & Limerick Borderlands, Ireland | Regional southwestern Irish dance music favoring brisk, accented 2/4 polkas and 12/8 slides over standard reels. |
| `atlantic-europe:28` | **Dutch Gabber (Rotterdam Hardcore)** | Rotterdam, Netherlands | Early-1990s extreme rave techno (160–200+ BPM) defined by overdriven, square-clipped Roland TR-909 bass kicks and hoover synth leads. |
| `atlantic-europe:29` | **Hague West Coast Electro** | The Hague, Netherlands | 1990s–present raw Dutch acid-electro combining Detroit Drexciyan aquatic rhythms with dusty analog Roland synthesizers and Italo-disco arpeggios. |
| `atlantic-europe:30` | **Alpine Jodel (Naturjodel) & Ländler** | Appenzell & Muotathal, Switzerland / Tyrol, Austria | Untexted polyphonic natural-scale yodeling and rustic clarinet/schwyzerörgeli (accordion) 3/4 folk dance music. |

## Research leads

| Route ID | Source label | Claimed origin / scene — verify | Claimed sonic profile — verify |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `mediterranean-europe:01` | **Italian Giallo Soundtracks & Library Music** | Rome & Milan, Italy | Late-1960s–70s cinematic suspense and lounge cues featuring propulsive Fender bass, harpsichord, Mellotron, wordless Edda Dell'Orso-style vocals, and analog synths. |
| `mediterranean-europe:02` | **Italo Disco** | Milan, Rimini, Bologna & Rome, Italy | Early-1980s fully electronic dance music driven by LinnDrum/Simmons percussion, octave-jumping Roland Juno/Jupiter basslines, and bittersweet English-accented vocals. |
| `mediterranean-europe:03` | **Balearic Beat (Ibiza Chug)** | Ibiza, Balearic Islands, Spain | Mid-1980s open-air terrace DJ aesthetic blending chugging mid-tempo European synth-pop, acoustic guitar instrumentals, dub, and proto-house. |
| `mediterranean-europe:04` | **Flamenco Jondo (Cante Jondo)** | Andalusia (Jerez, Seville, Cádiz, Granada), Spain | Deep, tragic Andalusian Romani-Spanish art form built on 12-beat compás cycles (soleá, seguiriyas), Phrygian guitar falsetas, palmas, and raw vocals. |
| `mediterranean-europe:05` | **Rumba Catalana** | Raval & Gràcia, Barcelona, Catalonia, Spain | 1950s–60s Catalan Romani fusion of flamenco guitar (using the percussive 'ventilador' slap-strum), Afro-Cuban guaracha/mambo, and rock and roll. |
| `mediterranean-europe:06` | **Bakalao (La Ruta Destroy)** | Valencia, Spain | 1980s–early-1990s Valencian marathon club scene combining Belgian EBM, British post-punk, guitar-driven synth-wave, and hard techno. |
| `mediterranean-europe:07` | **Galician Muiñeira & Celtic Gaita Folk** | Galicia & Asturias, Northwestern Spain | Atlantic Iberian 6/8 bagpipe (gaita gallega), tamboril snare, and pandereta tambourine dance music. |
| `mediterranean-europe:08` | **Basque Trikitixa & Ezpata-Dantza** | Basque Country (Euskadi & Navarre), Spain / France | Fast diatonic button accordion (trikitixa) and pandero tambourine duets alongside txistu (three-hole flute) and tabor dances. |
| `mediterranean-europe:09` | **Portuguese Fado (Fado de Lisboa & Coimbra)** | Alfama / Mouraria (Lisbon) & Coimbra, Portugal | Mournful urban romance sung with expressive rubato phrasing accompanied by the 12-string pear-shaped guitarra portuguesa and classical viola. |
| `mediterranean-europe:10` | **Cante Alentejano** | Alentejo Region, Southern Portugal | Unaccompanied two-part communal choral singing led by the ponto and alto soloists with powerful, resonant rural harmonies. |
| `mediterranean-europe:11` | **Kuduro Progressivo / Batida de Lisboa** | Quinta do Mocho & Amadora, Greater Lisbon, Portugal | Afro-Portuguese electronic club scene blending Angolan kuduro and tarraxinha rhythms with raw MPC sampling and synth bass. |
| `mediterranean-europe:12` | **Sardinian Canto a Tenore** | Barbagia Region, Sardinia, Italy | Ancient four-voice pastoral polyphony where three singers form a guttural overtone chord (boche, contra, bassu, mesu boche) around a solo lead. |
| `mediterranean-europe:13` | **Corsican Cantu in Paghjella** | Corsica, France | Three-voice secular and liturgical male polyphony combining staggered vocal entries (seconda, bassu, terza) with rich picardy-third cadences. |
| `mediterranean-europe:14` | **Southern Italian Pizzica & Tarantella Salentina** | Salento (Apulia), Campania & Calabria, Italy | Frenzied ritual healing dance music driven by relentless tamburello frame drums, violin, organetto accordion, and circular vocal chants. |
| `mediterranean-europe:15` | **Neapolitan Jazz-Funk (Napule's Power)** | Naples, Italy | 1970s–80s Mediterranean groove movement blending Neapolitan dialect vocals and Arabic-inflected scales with American jazz-funk and fusion. |
| `mediterranean-europe:16` | **Calabrian Canti di Malavita** | Calabria, Southern Italy | Traditional Southern Italian underworld narrative ballads accompanied by chitarra battente (wire-strung guitar) and organetto. |
| `mediterranean-europe:17` | **Greek Rebetiko (Piraeus & Smyrna Styles)** | Piraeus, Athens & Thessaloniki, Greece | Early-20th-century urban refugee underworld music played on three-course trichordo bouzouki, baglamas, and tzouras in modal dromoi scales. |
| `mediterranean-europe:18` | **Epirote Polyphonic Song & Miroloi** | Epirus (Pogoni), Northwestern Greece & Southern Albania | Pentatonic vocal polyphony and weeping clarinet instrumental laments (miroloi) played with slow, bending microtonal ornamentation. |
| `mediterranean-europe:19` | **Cretan Kritika (Lyra & Laouto)** | Crete, Greece | Virtuosic modal dance suites (pentozali, sousta, syrtos) pairing the bowed three-string Cretan lyra with percussive laouto lute chords. |
| `mediterranean-europe:20` | **Albanian Iso-Polyphony** | Tosk & Labëria Regions, Southern Albania | Multi-part vocal tradition anchored by a continuous collective vocal drone ('iso') supporting two weaving melodic soloists. |
| `mediterranean-europe:21` | **Bosnian Sevdalinka** | Sarajevo & Mostar, Bosnia and Herzegovina | Melancholic, emotionally intense urban love song combining Slavic lyricism and Ottoman makam modes with accordion or saz accompaniment. |
| `mediterranean-europe:22` | **Yugoslav Novi Val (Yugo New Wave)** | Zagreb, Belgrade, Ljubljana & Rijeka (Former Yugoslavia) | Late-1970s–early-1980s socialist post-punk, ska, and synth-art-rock celebrated for sharp irony and modernist guitar work. |
| `mediterranean-europe:23` | **Balkan Brass (Trubači / Čoček)** | Guča & Southern Serbia / North Macedonia | Romani and Serbian brass bands playing blistering odd-meter (7/8, 9/8, 11/8) čoček dances with screaming trumpets and helicon bass. |
| `mediterranean-europe:24` | **Macedonian Čalgija & Odd-Meter Oro** | Skopje, Bitola & Ohrid, North Macedonia | Urban Ottoman-Balkan chamber ensemble (oud, kanun, clarinet, violin, daire) performing intricate 7/8 and 11/8 dance meters. |
| `mediterranean-europe:25` | **Bulgarian Polyphonic Diaphony (Shopi & Pirin)** | Shopi Region & Pirin Mountains, Bulgaria | Piercing women's vocal polyphony utilizing dissonant narrow second intervals, chest-voice projection, and glottal ornaments (tresene). |
| `mediterranean-europe:26` | **Bulgarian Chalga / Wedding Music (Svatbarska Muzika)** | Thrace & Sofia, Bulgaria | High-velocity 1980s–present amplified Balkan fusion featuring microtonal clarinet/accordion shredding over complex asymmetric meters. |
| `mediterranean-europe:27` | **Romanian Lăutărească & Manele** | Bucharest & Wallachia, Romania | Romani professional string-and-cimbalom ensemble music (taraf) and its modern electronic oriental-pop descendant (manele). |
| `mediterranean-europe:28` | **Croatian Klapa Singing** | Dalmatia Coast & Islands, Croatia | Unaccompanied multi-part homophonic coastal singing celebrating maritime life, love, and Adriatic harmonies. |
| `mediterranean-europe:29` | **Slovenian Oberkrainer Polka & Neue Slowenische Kunst Industrial** | Gorenjska & Ljubljana, Slovenia | Contrasting Slovenian traditions: Alpine accordion-trumpet-clarinet quintet polkas and Laibach's monumental martial-industrial avant-garde. |
| `mediterranean-europe:30` | **Maltese Għana (Għana Spirtu Pront)** | Malta | Improvised, high-pitched microtonal dual vocal repartee accompanied by twin Spanish guitars playing ornate Prejjem interludes. |

| Route ID | Source label | Claimed origin / scene — verify | Claimed sonic profile — verify |

| `northern-europe:01` | **Norwegian Hardanger Fiddle (Hardingfele Slått)** | Telemark, Hardanger & Setesdal, Norway | Eight- or nine-string fiddle music using four under-strung sympathetic strings to play asymmetric springar and gangar dances. |
| `northern-europe:02` | **Sámi Joik (Luohti) & Contemporary Electric Joik** | Sápmi (Northern Norway, Sweden, Finland) | Vocal art form that evokes the essence of a person, animal, or landscape through pentatonic/hexatonic guttural and chest-voice phrasing. |
| `northern-europe:03` | **Norwegian Second-Wave Black Metal** | Oslo & Bergen, Norway | Early-1990s extreme metal defined by lo-fi 'necro' production, high-register tremolo-picked minor chords, blast beats, and shrieked vocals. |
| `northern-europe:04` | **Bergen Wave (Scandinavian Indie-Synth & Nu-Disco)** | Bergen & Tromsø, Norway | Late-1990s–2000s Norwegian scene pairing warm cosmic analog disco-house (Lindstrøm/Prins Thomas style) with hushed acoustic indie pop. |
| `northern-europe:05` | **Nordic Nu-Jazz (Bugge Wesseltoft / ECM Fjord Jazz)** | Oslo, Norway | Spacious, chamber-like Scandinavian jazz merging acoustic piano and breathy trumpet with live electronic loops and ambient sub-bass. |
| `northern-europe:06` | **Swedish Polska (Slängpolska & Hambo)** | Dalarna & Hälsingland, Sweden | 3/4 fiddle and nyckelharpa (keyed fiddle) dance music characterized by an uneven, weighted second beat and drone strings. |
| `northern-europe:07` | **Swedish Kulning (Herding Call)** | Dalarna & Jämtland Forest Pastures, Sweden | High-decibel, vibrato-free female high-register vocal call used to summon livestock across kilometres of mountain forest. |
| `northern-europe:08` | **Gothenburg Melodic Death Metal** | Gothenburg, Sweden | early-1990s fusion of brutal death metal vocals/percussion with twin-guitar Iron Maiden-style NWOBHM melodic harmonies. |
| `northern-europe:09` | **Stockholm Buzzsaw Death Metal (HM-2 Sound)** | Stockholm, Sweden | Late-1980s–90s death metal built around the Boss HM-2 Heavy Metal pedal turned to maximum for a chainsaw guitar timbre. |
| `northern-europe:10` | **Swedish Trallpunk & D-Beat (Kängpunk)** | Gothenburg, Umeå & Avesta, Sweden | Fast, driving hardcore punk driven by Discharge-derived D-beat rhythms or hyper-melodic sing-along Swedish punk choruses. |
| `northern-europe:11` | **Skweee** | Stockholm, Sweden & Helsinki, Finland | Mid-2000s Nordic instrumental synth-funk combining bare-bones analog lead melodies with wobbly mid-tempo R&B/electro basslines. |
| `northern-europe:12` | **Finnish Runolaulu & Kantele Music** | Karelia & Eastern Finland | Ancient Kalevala-meter (trochaic tetrameter) pentatonic chanting accompanied by the plucked five-to-thirty-nine-string kantele zither. |
| `northern-europe:13` | **Finnish Tango (Suomalainen Tango)** | Seinäjoki & Satakunta, Finland | Melancholic, minor-key Nordic adaptation of tango featuring accordion, steady march-to-habanera shifts, and stoic nature imagery. |
| `northern-europe:14` | **Suomisaundi (Spugedelic Trance)** | Helsinki & Turku, Finland | Mid-1990s freeform, playful Finnish psychedelic trance incorporating video-game chiptunes, funk guitar, and eccentric melodic turns. |
| `northern-europe:15` | **Finnish Rautalanka (Twist & Shadows-Style Surf)** | Helsinki & Tampere, Finland | Early-1960s–present Finnish clean-toned Fender Stratocaster instrumental guitar music adapting traditional Slavic/Finnish minor melodies. |
| `northern-europe:16` | **Finnish Humppa & Jenkka** | Ostrobothnia & Karelia, Finland | Bouncy, syncopated two-step foxtrot (humppa) and rustic polka-schottische (jenkka) played on accordion, fiddle, and upright bass. |
| `northern-europe:17` | **Icelandic Rímur & Tvísöngur** | Iceland | Epic alliterative ballad chanting (rímur) and stark medieval parallel-fifth two-part vocal harmony (tvísöngur) with crossing voice parts. |
| `northern-europe:18` | **Reykjavík Post-Rock & Glacial Chamber Pop** | Reykjavík, Iceland | Atmospheric Icelandic music using cello bows on electric guitars, brass choirs, falsetto vocals, and vast dynamic swells. |
| `northern-europe:19` | **Faroese Kvøði (Chain Dance Ballad)** | Faroe Islands | Unaccompanied narrative ballad singing where a lead cantor (skipari) and stamping circle dancers perform hundreds of medieval verses. |
| `northern-europe:20` | **Danish Spillemandsmusik (Sønderhoning & Pols)** | Fanø Island & Jutland, Denmark | Coastal Danish fiddle and accordion dance music featuring subtle rhythmic drag and asymmetric bowing. |
| `northern-europe:21` | **Copenhagen Fastbeat & 80s Minimal Punk** | Copenhagen, Denmark | Late-1970s–80s Danish post-punk and dark minimal wave centered around the Rockmaskinen and Sort Sol underground. |
| `northern-europe:22` | **Lithuanian Sutartinės** | Aukštaitija (Northeastern Lithuania) | Polyphonic women's multipart vocal canons and rounds featuring interlocking syllabic vocables and pungent second-interval clashes. |
| `northern-europe:23` | **Latvian Dainas & Kokle Drone Music** | Riga, Kurzeme & Latgale, Latvia | Quatrain folk songs (dainas) accompanied by the hollowed wooden kokle box zither and drone vocal harmonies. |
| `northern-europe:24` | **Estonian Regilaul (Runo Song) & Seto Leelo** | Setomaa & Coastal Estonia | Ancient Finnic alliterative call-and-response choral singing featuring a piercing high solo voice (killõ) over a dense choir. |
| `northern-europe:25` | **Baltic Choral Minimalism (Tintinnabuli)** | Tallinn, Estonia & Riga, Latvia | Sacred and secular choral-orchestral style pioneered by Arvo Pärt and Veljo Tormis using bell-like triadic resonance and silence. |
| `northern-europe:26` | **Umeå Straight-Edge Hardcore** | Umeå, Northern Sweden | 1990s militant vegan/straight-edge hardcore scene (Refused era) blending jazz-fusion breaks and electronic interludes with hardcore punk. |
| `northern-europe:27` | **Norwegian Trønderrock** | Trondheim & Trøndelag, Norway | 1970s–present regional Norwegian roots-rock sung in Trøndersk dialect with accordion, fiddle, and pub-rock guitars. |
| `northern-europe:28` | **Finnish Funeral Doom Metal** | Turku & Jyväskylä, Finland | Ultra-slow (40–60 BPM) extreme doom metal pairing glacial distorted guitars and funeral organ with cavernous growls. |
| `northern-europe:29` | **Swedish Progg (Musikrörelsen)** | Stockholm, Gothenburg & Uppsala, Sweden | Late-1960s–70s anti-commercial left-wing Swedish psychedelic folk-rock fusing traditional polska fiddles with electric fuzz jams. |
| `northern-europe:30` | **Danish Balearic Synth & 80s Boogie** | Copenhagen & Aarhus, Denmark | Breezy, jazz-chorded Scandinavian yacht-boogie and analog synth-pop from Denmark's mid-1980s studio renaissance. |

# END SOURCE: references/regions/northern-europe.md

| Route ID | Source label | Claimed origin / scene — verify | Claimed sonic profile — verify |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `eastern-europe:01` | **Soviet Estrada-Funk & VIA Groove** | Moscow, Leningrad, Minsk & Riga (Former USSR) | 1970s State-sanctioned Vocal-Instrumental Ensembles (VIAs) smuggling fuzz guitars, Clavinet funk, and jazz-rock breaks into pop songs. |
| `eastern-europe:02` | **Soviet Cosmic Synth & Radiophonic Electronica** | Moscow (Scriabin Museum ANS Studio) & Riga | 1960s–80s space-age electronic music built on the photoelectric ANS synthesizer, Polivoks analog synths, and sci-fi film scores. |
| `eastern-europe:03` | **Leningrad Underground Post-Punk (Rock Club Scene)** | Leningrad / Saint Petersburg, Russia | 1980s Soviet new wave and post-punk (Kino/Auktyon era) combining drum machines, chorus-drenched guitars, and existential Russian poetry. |
| `eastern-europe:04` | **Belarusian Folk-Prog (Pesnyary Era)** | Minsk, Belarus | 1970s–80s progressive folk-rock synthesis blending archaic Belarusian polyphonic vocal harmonies, lira hurdy-gurdy, and dulcimer with Hammond organ and fuzz guitar. |
| `eastern-europe:05` | **Russian Blatnaya Pesnya (Odessa / Urban Underworld Song)** | Odessa & Urban Soviet Periphery | Early-to-mid-20th-century klezmer-and-gypsy-inflected outlaw and thieves' cabaret songs featuring clarinet, violin, and acoustic guitar. |
| `eastern-europe:06` | **Russian Bard Song (Avtorskaya Pesnya)** | Moscow & Soviet Kitchen/Campfire Networks | 1960s–70s intimate, lyric-driven seven-string acoustic guitar poetry circulated via reel-to-reel magnitizdat tapes. |
| `eastern-europe:07` | **Polish Jazz School (Komeda / Polish Modern Jazz)** | Kraków, Warsaw & Sopot, Poland | Late-1950s–70s avant-garde and modal jazz movement blending Slavic romanticism and film scoring with post-bop improvisation. |
| `eastern-europe:08` | **Polish Zimna Fala (Cold Wave)** | Warsaw, Łódź, Gdańsk & Jarocin, Poland | 1980s martial-law-era Polish post-punk and darkwave marked by stark basslines, dub echo, saxophones, and coded political lyrics. |
| `eastern-europe:09` | **Polish Górale (Podhale Highland String Music)** | Tatra Mountains (Zakopane / Podhale), Southern Poland | High-energy mountain string quartet music playing the Lydian-fourth 'Podhale scale' with screaming lead fiddle and three-string basy. |
| `eastern-europe:10` | **Mazovian Oberki & Polish Rural Roots Revival** | Mazovia & Radom Region, Central Poland | Trance-like, whirling 3/8 rural fiddle and baraban (drum with cymbal) dance music using microtonal notes and rubato phrasing. |
| `eastern-europe:11` | **Czech Underground (Plastic People Era Avant-Rock)** | Prague, Czechia | 1970s dissident psychedelic art-rock fusing Velvet Underground viola drones, Frank Zappa freak-outs, and dark Czech poetry. |
| `eastern-europe:12` | **Moravian Cimbálová Muzika** | South Moravia (Slovácko), Czechia | Wine-region string and cimbalom (hammered dulcimer) ensemble music characterized by Moravian modal inflections and free-tempo songs. |
| `eastern-europe:13` | **Slovak Fujara Pastoral Music & Terchová String Music** | Detva & Terchová, Central Slovakia | Deep overtone flute (fujara) shepherd solos and high-energy multiphonic highland string ensembles. |
| `eastern-europe:14` | **Hungarian Táncház (Dance-House) String Revival** | Budapest, Hungary & Transylvania | 1970s–present urban revival of rural Hungarian and Transylvanian village string-band music featuring the three-bridge kontra viola. |
| `eastern-europe:15` | **Magyar Nóta & Verbunkos (Hungarian Café / Recruiting Dance)** | Budapest, Hungary | 19th-to-20th-century Romani string and cimbalom ensemble style alternating slow rubato laments (hallgató) with fiery csárdás accelerations. |
| `eastern-europe:16` | **Ukrainian Koliadky & Polyphonic White-Voice Song** | Polissia & Podillia, Ukraine | Archaic pre-Christian winter solstice carols and open-throat ('bilyi holos') polyphonic village singing. |
| `eastern-europe:17` | **Ukrainian Kobzarstvo & Bandura Epic Tradition** | Poltava, Chernihiv & Kharkiv, Ukraine | Epic historical ballads (dumy) sung by itinerant bards playing the asymmetric multi-stringed bandura or kobza lute. |
| `eastern-europe:18` | **Ukrainian Vusatyi Funk (Moustache Funk)** | Kyiv, Lviv & Chernivtsi, Ukraine | 1970s Ukrainian psychedelic funk-rock merging Carpathian folk polyphony and horn sections with heavy fuzz guitar and breakbeats. |
| `eastern-europe:19` | **Hutsul Carpathian Arkan & Trembita Music** | Ivano-Frankivsk & Bukovina (Carpathian Mountains), Ukraine | Highland shepherd music featuring three-meter birchbark trembita horns, tsymbaly dulcimer, drymba jaw harp, and sopilka flutes. |
| `eastern-europe:20` | **Belarusian Kupalle & Polesie Archaic Polyphony** | Polesie Wetlands, Southern Belarus | Swamp-village ritual calendar singing preserving heterophonic drones, whooping vocal calls (hukanne), and narrow-interval harmonies. |
| `eastern-europe:21` | **Moldovan Joc & Fluier-Caval Shepherd Music** | Chișinău & Rural Moldova | Fast-paced hora, sârbă, and bătuta circle dances played on end-blown fluier, low caval flute, nai panpipes, and cobza lute. |
| `eastern-europe:22` | **Klezmer (Bessarabian & Podolian Ashkenazi String/Clarinet)** | Bessarabia, Podolia, Galicia & Bukovina | Eastern European Jewish wedding instrumental music featuring expressive clarinet/violin 'krekhts' (sobbing ornaments) over bulgar and freylekhs rhythms. |
| `eastern-europe:23` | **Cossack Polyphonic Choir (Don & Kuban Cossack Song)** | Don & Kuban River Basins, Southern Russia / Ukraine | Powerful, wide-interval multi-part male choral singing featuring a soaring tenor discant line improvised above the ensemble. |
| `eastern-europe:24` | **Tatar Jazz & Pentatonic Estrada** | Kazan, Tatarstan (Volga Region) | Volga Tatar musical tradition blending anhemitonic pentatonic folk melodies and bayan accordion with big-band jazz and synth-funk. |
| `eastern-europe:25` | **Mordvin (Erzya & Moksha) Torama Polyphony** | Mordovia, Volga Basin | Finno-Ugric multi-part vocal polyphony accompanied by long birchbark trumpets (torama) and reed pipes. |
| `eastern-europe:26` | **Udmurt Kreź & Buranovskiye Folk Chants** | Udmurtia (Kama-Volga Basin) | Finno-Ugric ritual improvised melodies played on the zither-like kreź and sung in collective village rounds. |
| `eastern-europe:27` | **Post-Soviet Doomer Post-Punk (Minsk / Moscow Coldwave Revival)** | Minsk, Belarus & Moscow / Yekaterinburg, Russia | 2010s–2020s lo-fi Eastern European coldwave combining LinnDrum/TR-707 beats, chorus-saturated basslines, and Brutalist urban melancholy. |
| `eastern-europe:28` | **Russian Hardbass** | Saint Petersburg & Moscow, Russia | Early-2000s 150–160 BPM pumping house/techno derivative defined by the metallic 'donk' FM bass offbeat and tongue-in-cheek street chants. |
| `eastern-europe:29` | **Chuvash & Mari Gusli (Küsle) Pentatonic Song** | Mari El & Chuvashia, Middle Volga | Delicate helmet-shaped psaltery (küsle) and bagpipe (shüvyr) music accompanying pentatonic nature songs. |
| `eastern-europe:30` | **Transylvanian Csángó Flute & Koboz Dance Music** | Moldavia / Gyimes (Eastern Carpathians), Romania | Archaic Hungarian-speaking Csángó village music driven by six-hole shepherd flute, percussive koboz lute, and gardon (struck cello). |

| `north-africa:02` | **Moroccan Ghiwane (Nass El Ghiwane Folk-Trance Revival)** | Hay Mohammadi, Casablanca, Morocco | 1970s revolutionary acoustic roots movement fusing Gnawa guembri and Sufi Aissawa rhythms with banjo, bendir, and protest poetry. |
| `north-africa:03` | **Amazigh Rwaïs & Ahwash** | Souss Valley & Anti-Atlas Mountains, Morocco | Tashelhit-language professional poet-musician ensembles playing the one-string ribab fiddle and metallic lotar lute. |
| `north-africa:04` | **Atlas Amazigh Ahidous & Lotar Groove** | Middle Atlas (Khenifra & Azrou), Morocco | Collective shoulder-to-shoulder circle dance accompanied by bendir frame drums and electrified four-string lotar lutes. |
| `north-africa:05` | **Moroccan Chaabi (Chaabi Marocain)** | Casablanca, Rabat & Marrakech, Morocco | High-energy urban and wedding celebratory music driven by virtuosic violin (kamanja), darbouka, bendir, and plucked oud or electric guitar. |
| `north-africa:06` | **Algerian Raï (Pop-Raï & Synth-Raï)** | Oran & Sidi Bel Abbès, Algeria | Rebellious western Algerian music evolving from bedouin gasba flutes to 1980s Roland synthesizers, drum machines, fuzz guitar, and Cheb/Chaba vocals. |
| `north-africa:07` | **Algerian Chaabi** | Casbah of Algiers, Algeria | Poetic urban folk music derived from Andalusian classical modes, anchored by the flat-backed Algerian mandole, banjo, darbouka, and qanun. |
| `north-africa:08` | **Kabyle Music (Chanson Kabyle)** | Kabylia Mountains (Tizi Ouzou & Béjaïa), Algeria | Amazigh Algerian song tradition featuring intricate mandole fingerpicking, darbouka percussion, and poetic social-justice lyrics. |
| `north-africa:09` | **Sahrawi Haul & Tiris Electric Guitar** | Western Sahara & Tindouf Refugee Camps | Desert modal music built on eight progressive spiritual/social modes (haul), combining tidinit lute traditions with overdriven electric guitar and tbal drum. |
| `north-africa:10` | **Tuareg Assouf (Ishumar / Desert Guitar Blues)** | Tamanrasset (Algeria), Kidal (Mali) & Agadez (Niger) | Hypnotic, cyclical electric guitar music rooted in Tuareg teherdent lute and tende drum rhythms, forged by displaced Saharan youth. |
| `north-africa:11` | **Tuareg Tende & Imzad Music** | Hoggar, Tassili n'Ajjer & Aïr Mountains | Matriarchal Tuareg music featuring the mortar-pestle water drum (tende) with handclapping choruses and the bowed single-string imzad violin. |
| `north-africa:12` | **Tunisian Stambeli** | Tunis & Sousse, Tunisia | Afro-Tunisian healing trance music pairing the deep-voiced gumbrī lute and shqashiq iron clappers with spirit-invocation melodies. |
| `north-africa:13` | **Tunisian Mezwed** | Tunis Working-Class Neighborhoods, Tunisia | Raw, festive urban street music powered by the goatskin bagpipe (mezwed), booming bendir/darbouka percussion, and emotional street vocals. |
| `north-africa:14` | **Andalusian Ma'luf (Malouf)** | Tunis, Testour & Constantine (Tunisia / Eastern Algeria) | North African classical suite tradition preserved from medieval Al-Andalus, played on oud, rabab, nay, violin, and tar. |
| `north-africa:15` | **Libyan Reggae (Ibrahim Hesnawi Scene)** | Tripoli & Benghazi, Libya | Late-1970s–80s Libyan adaptation of Jamaican roots reggae using Arabic lyrics, analog organs, and Mediterranean guitar phrasing. |
| `north-africa:16` | **Libyan Merskawi & Cassette Pop** | Murzuk (Fezzan) & Benghazi, Libya | Pentatonic-meets-maqam Libyan oasis and coastal song tradition recorded in the 1970s–80s with electric organ, accordion, and hand drums. |
| `north-africa:17` | **Egyptian Al-Jeel** | Cairo & Alexandria, Egypt | Late-1970s–80s Egyptian synth-pop and disco-funk pioneered by Hamid El Shaeri, blending Libyan/Egyptian rhythms with quarter-tone synthesizers. |
| `north-africa:18` | **Egyptian Mahraganat (Electro-Chaabi)** | El Salam City & Dar El Salam, Cairo, Egypt | 2010s street-wedding electronic music pairing frantic FruityLoops darbouka loops and high-pitched mizmar synth hooks with Auto-Tuned rap. |
| `north-africa:19` | **Egyptian Sa'idi Mizmar & Tahtib Music** | Upper Egypt (Luxor, Qena, Asyut) | Powerful Upper Egyptian double-reed shawm (mizmar) and large tabl baladi bass drum music accompanying traditional stick-fighting dances. |
| `north-africa:20` | **Nubian Pentatonic Oud & Arghul Groove** | Aswan (Southern Egypt) & Wadi Halfa (Northern Sudan) | Loping, syncopated Nile Valley music sung in Kenzi and Fadija Nubian languages with oud, duff frame drums, and electric bass. |
| `north-africa:21` | **Egyptian Zār Ritual Percussion** | Cairo & Nile Delta, Egypt | Women-led spirit-pacification ceremony driven by the six-string tambura lyre and polyrhythmic mangour (hoof-belt) and mazhar drums. |
| `north-africa:22` | **Sudanese Haqiba & Khartoum Jazz (Sharhabil Era)** | Omdurman & Khartoum, Sudan | 1950s–70s urban Sudanese golden-age band music blending pentatonic vocal melodies with saxophones, brass, bongos, and electric guitars. |
| `north-africa:23` | **Sudanese Jaglara (Zanig)** | Khartoum & Omdurman, Sudan | High-speed (~130–150 BPM) contemporary Sudanese underground keyboard and drum-machine street music played at neighborhood parties. |
| `north-africa:24` | **Sudanese Tape-Era Synth-Orchestra (Sawt El-Sudan)** | Khartoum, Sudan | 1970s–80s cassette-era Sudanese songs replacing big-band brass with polyphonic analog synthesizers and syncopated tum-tum rhythms. |
| `north-africa:25` | **Beja Basankob (Tambura Rababa Music)** | Red Sea Hills & Port Sudan (Eastern Sudan / Egypt) | Cushitic Beja nomadic music centered on the five-string plucked rababa lyre, foot-stomping rhythms, and poetic camel-herding songs. |
| `north-africa:26` | **Mauritanian Griot Music (Azawan / Ardin & Tidinit)** | Nouakchott & Adrar, Mauritania | Complex Moorish griot (iggawin) modal system progressing from white to black modes, played on the female 14-string ardin harp and male tidinit lute / electric guitar. |
| `north-africa:27` | **Siwa Oasis Amazigh Bridal & Date-Harvest Music** | Siwa Oasis, Western Desert, Egypt | Distinctive easternmost Amazigh oasis polyrhythms featuring pottery drums, flute, and call-and-response group clapping. |
| `north-africa:28` | **Suez Canal Simsimiyya** | Port Said, Ismailia & Suez, Egypt | Coastal canal workers' music centered on the bright, plucked five-to-twenty-string simsimiyya box lyre and maritime spoon percussion. |
| `north-africa:29` | **Moroccan Reggada & Aarfa** | Berkane, Oujda & Rif Mountains, Northeastern Morocco | Warrior shoulder-dance music driven by the zamar (double-horn pipe with bull horns), gasba reed flute, and pounding bendir drums. |
| `north-africa:30` | **Algerian Diwan** | Béchar, Saïda & Biskra, Algeria | Saharan Algerian spiritual brotherhood music combining sub-Saharan pentatonic gumbri bass grooves with karkabou iron clappers. |

| `west-africa:10` | **Kpanlogo & Ga Gome Percussion** | Greater Accra Coast, Ghana | Urban Ga youth recreational drumming combining barrel-shaped kpanlogo congas, giant square frame drums (gome), and iron bells. |
| `west-africa:11` | **Malian Mandé Griot (Jeliya) Kora & Ngoni Music** | Bamako, Kita & Kangaba, Mali | Hereditary jeli praise-singing accompanied by cascading 21-string kora harp-lute, kontingo/ngoni lute, and wooden balafon. |
| `west-africa:12` | **Wassoulou Music** | Sikasso Region (Southern Mali) | Female-led hunters' harp (kamalen ngoni) music driven by a funky pentatonic six-string bass groove, karinyan metal scraper, and soaring vocals. |
| `west-africa:13` | **Takamba** | Gao & Timbuktu (Niger River Bend), Northern Mali | Hypnotic Songhai and Tuareg sedentary/nomadic music played in asymmetric meters on amplified ngoni lutes and calabash percussion. |
| `west-africa:14` | **Malian Balani Show (Electro Balani)** | Bamako, Mali | High-tempo street block-party electronic music layering rapid djembe and sabar samples over distorted digital marimba/balafon loops. |
| `west-africa:15` | **Senegalese Mbalax** | Dakar, Senegal | Explosive urban dance music fusing Wolof sabar and tama talking-drum polyrhythms with Cuban pachanga horns and sharp electric guitars. |
| `west-africa:16` | **Senegalese Afro-Cuban Salsa (Orchestra Baobab Era)** | Dakar & Casamance, Senegal | 1970s Dakar nightclub synthesis marrying Cuban son montuno and pachanga with Casamance and Wolof guitar modes. |
| `west-africa:17` | **Guinean Manding Big-Band Jazz (Bembeya Jazz Era)** | Conakry & Beyla, Guinea | Post-independence state orchestra sound adapting centuries-old Mandinka balafon and kora epics to electric guitars and brass sections. |
| `west-africa:18` | **Cape Verdean Funaná** | Santiago Island, Cabo Verde | Fast, rebellious rural dance music played on the two-row diatonic gaita accordion and scraped iron ferrinho bar (and later 1980s synthesizers). |
| `west-africa:19` | **Cape Verdean Morna & Coladeira** | Mindelo, São Vicente, Cabo Verde | Bittersweet, nostalgic island balladry (morna) and its upbeat sibling (coladeira) played on cavaquinho, 10-string viola, violin, and clarinet. |
| `west-africa:20` | **Batuque & Tabanka** | Santiago Island, Cabo Verde | Women's polyrhythmic circle music where singers beat rolled cloth cushions (tchabéta) held between their thighs in 2-against-3 cross-rhythms. |
| `west-africa:21` | **Ivorian Coupé-Décalé** | Abidjan, Côte d'Ivoire & Paris Diaspora | Early-2000s high-octane electronic percussion dance music featuring rolling tom-tom programming, atalaku hype-shouting, and celebratory flair. |
| `west-africa:22` | **Ivorian Ziglibithy & Zouglou** | Gagnoa & Abidjan University Campuses, Côte d'Ivoire | Bété-rooted polyrhythmic guitar funk (ziglibithy) and satirical student street-percussion harmony music (zouglou). |
| `west-africa:23` | **Beninese Vodun Polyphonic Funk (Poly-Rythmo Era)** | Cotonou & Porto-Novo, Benin | 1970s Beninese Afro-funk adapting sacred Sakpata and Sato Vodun ceremonial rhythms to fuzz guitars, organs, and James Brown horns. |
| `west-africa:24` | **Nigerien Tal National / Hausa-Songhai Wedding Guitar** | Niamey & Zinder, Niger | Relentless, five-hour amplified wedding-band music blending Hausa talking drums, Fulani flutes, and intricate high-speed guitar picking. |
| `west-africa:25` | **Sierra Leonean Gumbe & Milo Jazz** | Freetown, Sierra Leone | Krio square-frame carpenter-drum music (gumbe) descending from Jamaican Maroon settlers, played with bottle-and-triangle percussion. |
| `west-africa:26` | **Sierra Leonean Bubu Music** | Northern Sierra Leone & Freetown | Ancient bamboo-tube blowing procession music modernized in the 1990s by Janka Nabay with Casio keyboards and drum machines. |
| `west-africa:27` | **Gambian Mandinka Kora & Yeyengo** | Banjul & Brikama, The Gambia | Coastal Gambian griot kora mastery and ndaga/mbalax-adjacent percussion ensembles. |
| `west-africa:28` | **Togolese Agbadza-Funk & Lomé Afro-Soul** | Lomé, Togo | 1970s Togolese Ewe rhythm adaptations blending traditional 12/8 agbadza bell patterns with soulful female vocals and organ. |
| `west-africa:29` | **Bissau-Guinean Gumbe** | Bissau, Guinea-Bissau | Lusophone West African syncopated guitar-and-calabash dance groove driven by the sikó frame drum and tina water drum. |
| `west-africa:30` | **Burkinabé Bobo Balafon & Volta Jazz-Funk** | Bobo-Dioulasso & Ouagadougou, Burkina Faso | Pentatonic wooden xylophone ensembles with buzzing spider-egg-sac resonators and 1970s Upper Volta electric horn bands. |

## Research leads

| Route ID | Source label | Claimed origin / scene — verify | Claimed sonic profile — verify |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `east-africa:01` | **Habesha Ethio-Jazz & Tizita Groove** | Addis Ababa, Ethiopia | 1960s–70s Swinging Addis instrumental jazz and soul built on Ethiopian pentatonic modes (tizita, bati, ambassel) with vibraphone, organ, and sax. |
| `east-africa:02` | **Ethiopian Azmari Masinko & Krar Song** | Gondar, Bahir Dar & Addis Ababa, Ethiopia | Improvised, double-entendre tavern poetry sung by azmari minstrels playing the one-string bowed masinko fiddle or six-string krar lyre. |
| `east-africa:03` | **Eritrean Guayla** | Asmara, Eritrea | Hypnotic Tigrinya circle-dance music propelled by the amplified five-string krar, kebero double-headed drum, and brass/synth lines. |
| `east-africa:04` | **Somali Qaraami & Mogadishu Funk (Dur-Dur Band Era)** | Mogadishu & Hargeisa, Somalia | Mid-20th-century Somali pentatonic oud love songs (qaraami) and their 1970s–80s hotel-band evolution featuring Farfisa organ and disco-funk bass. |
| `east-africa:05` | **Somali Diaspora 808 Xamar Synth-Pop** | Mogadishu & 1990s Jeddah/East African Diaspora | Cassette-era Somali music recorded on portable Yamaha/Roland keyboards and TR-808 drum machines with soulful vocal melisma. |
| `east-africa:06` | **Djibouti Broadcast Ensemble Groove (RTD Era)** | Djibouti City, Djibouti | 1970s–80s horn-and-synth state radio recordings blending Somali pentatonic melodies, Afar rhythms, reggae skanks, and Red Sea funk. |
| `east-africa:07` | **Kenyan Benga** | Lake Victoria (Nyanza) & Nairobi, Kenya | Fast-paced Luo-rooted dance music where the electric bass and three interlocking high-register guitars mimic the nyatiti eight-string lyre. |
| `east-africa:08` | **Luo Nyatiti & Orutu Music** | Kisumu & Siaya (Western Kenya) | Traditional Lake Victoria music pairing the plucked nyatiti lyre, toe-ring iron bells (gara), and one-string bowed orutu fiddle. |
| `east-africa:09` | **Mijikenda Chakacha & Sengenya** | Mombasa, Kilifi & Lamu (Kenyan Coast) | Swahili coastal 12/8 hip-swaying percussion music featuring mwaribe/msondo drums, nzumari oboe, and brass horns. |
| `east-africa:10` | **Kenyan Gengetone & Kapuka** | Eastlands, Nairobi, Kenya | Sheng-slang Nairobi street rap evolving from early-2000s kapuka bounce into minimalist, dancehall-inflected Gengetone club rhythms. |
| `east-africa:11` | **Tanzanian Muziki wa Dansi (Zilipendwa)** | Dar es Salaam, Tanzania | 1960s–80s Swahili big-band guitar rumba featuring twin saxophone harmonies, walking basslines, and poetic Swahili storytelling. |
| `east-africa:12` | **Zanzibari Taarab** | Stone Town, Zanzibar, Tanzania | Lush Indian Ocean orchestral tradition uniting Swahili sung poetry with Egyptian-style string sections, qanun, oud, accordion, and dumbak. |
| `east-africa:13` | **Kidumbak** | Zanzibar, Tanzania | Working-class stripped-down cousin of taarab driven by two small clay kidumbak drums, a single violin, sanduku (tea-chest bass), and cherewa. |
| `east-africa:14` | **Tanzanian Singeli** | Manzese & Kinondoni (Dar es Salaam), Tanzania | Hyper-speed (180–300 BPM) DIY electronic street music combining frantic pitched-up taarab/mchiriku loops with breathless Swahili MCing. |
| `east-africa:15` | **Wagogo Polyphonic Song & Ilzeze Music** | Dodoma Region (Central Tanzania) | Harmonic overtone singing and multi-part choral polyphony accompanied by the large multi-tined ilimba thumb piano and bowed zeze fiddle. |
| `east-africa:16` | **Ugandan Kadongo Kamu** | Kampala (Buganda Region), Uganda | Narrative 'one-little-guitar' storytelling music where a solo acoustic/electric bassist-guitarist sings lengthy moral and political ballads. |
| `east-africa:17` | **Northern Ugandan Acholi Larakaraka & Acholitronix** | Gulu & Kitgum, Northern Uganda | Acholi courtship calabash-and-lukeme (thumb piano) music and its modern electronic 'electro-acholi' keyboard adaptation. |
| `east-africa:18` | **Bugisu Kadodi Percussion** | Mbale (Mount Elgon Region), Eastern Uganda | Thunderous Bamasaaba circumcision-carnival drumming ensemble featuring interlocking calfskin drums, whistles, and horns. |
| `east-africa:19` | **Rwandan Inanga Whispering Zither Song** | Kigali & Nyanza, Rwanda | Intimate, breathy pentatonic vocal poetry accompanied by the trough-carved wooden inanga zither played in 5/8 and 7/8 meters. |
| `east-africa:20` | **Burundian Ingoma Royal Drumming & Akazehe** | Gitega, Burundi | Thunderous ritual ensemble of hollowed tree-trunk drums played on the head during royal ceremonies, alongside women's interlocking akazehe greeting songs. |
| `east-africa:21` | **Malagasy Salegy** | Antsiranana & Mahajanga (Coastal Madagascar) | Electrifying 6/8 and 12/8 coastal dance music featuring rollercoaster accordion/keyboard arpeggios, valiha-style guitar picking, and slap bass. |
| `east-africa:22` | **Malagasy Tsapiky** | Toliara (Southwestern Madagascar) | Relentless, high-velocity electric guitar party music translating traditional marovany box-zither patterns onto overdriven Fender guitars. |
| `east-africa:23` | **Malagasy Hira Gasy & Valiha Highland Music** | Antananarivo (Central Highlands), Madagascar | All-day rural operatic brass-and-fiddle theatre (hira gasy) and delicate tubular bamboo zither (valiha) cascades. |
| `east-africa:24` | **Mauritian Sega Tipik** | Mauritius | Afro-Mauritian coastal hearth music played on the goatskin ravanne frame drum heated over fire, maravanne rattle, and triangle. |
| `east-africa:25` | **Seggae** | Roche-Bois (Port Louis), Mauritius | Late-1980s fusion created by Kaya uniting the shuffling 6/8 ravanne pulse of Mauritian sega with Jamaican roots reggae. |
| `east-africa:26` | **Réunionnais Maloya** | Réunion Island | Sacred and protest music of enslaved ancestors sung in Réunion Creole over the roulèr barrel drum, kayamb reed raft shaker, and pikèr. |
| `east-africa:27` | **Rodrigues Sega Tambour & Chagossian Sega** | Rodrigues Island & Chagos Archipelago | High-tempo women-and-men frame-drum sega traditions featuring syncopated handclapping and diatonic accordion. |
| `east-africa:28` | **Comorian Twarab & Mgodro** | Moroni (Grande Comore) & Anjouan, Comoros | Swahili-Arab wedding orchestra music (twarab) and infectious 6/8 island dance rhythms played on gabusi lute and msondo drum. |
| `east-africa:29` | **Seychellois Moutya & Kontredans** | Mahé, Seychelles | Bonfire goatskin frame-drum music (moutya) and Franco-African violin/banjo quadrille reels (kontredans / kamtole). |
| `east-africa:30` | **South Sudanese Luo / Bari Lyre & Juba Brass Jazz** | Juba & Wau, South Sudan | 1970s–present southern Nile horn-and-guitar jazz bands blending Bari and Dinka vocal calls with Congolese rumba and reggae. |

| Route ID | Source label | Claimed origin / scene — verify | Claimed sonic profile — verify |
